# Izgil Lunar Sorcerer Early Design (Email)

Local archive of all 27 message bodies in this correspondence, including quoted reply history. Pre-campaign proposed lunar sorcerer rules and feedback.

## Thu, 29 Dec 2022 10:53:49 -0500 — Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com>

Subject: Lunar Sorceror

Matt,

I haven't made up more than the initial bits, but here is what I'm thinking
for lunar sorcerer. The version below is only made up through 6th level and
leans heavily into different abilities for different phases of the moon. I
am happy to set up a moon calendar so we know which phases of the moon are
which (probably by just picking an Earth year and using that) but I can't
quite decide if it will be too annoying to track.

What do you think of the below? I'm still tweaking a bit so might play with
the power levels a bit and totally happy to get feedback. If you like it
I'll make it in D&D Beyond.

*1st Level Power*
You gain a new cantrip, *moon's blessing*, that allows you to call forth
the power of the moon to attack your enemies, and additionally, you gain a
set of bonus spells.

*Moon's Blessing*
You call forth the blessing of the moon to attack your enemies. You choose
an enemy within 60', who must make a DEX save or take 1d8 damage. The spell
damage increases by 1d8 at 5th, 11th, and 17th level.

During the full moon, and the week around it, the lunar tides pull strongly
on the planes of the gods, and the damage is radiant, and you gain the
ability to target a second creature within 5'.

During the new moon, and the week around it, the lunar tides pull strongly
on the elemental planes, the damage is elemental, channeled from the
elemental planes. You may choose which plane to channel when you cast the
spell:

From the plane of fire, the spell does fire damage.
From the plane of water, the spell does cold damage.
From the plane of air, the spell does thunder or lightning damage, your
choice
From the plane of earth, the spell does bludgeoning damage and deals double
damage to object or structures

In between, when the moon is a crescent, the tides are balanced, and the
spell does force damage, and has a range of 90' instead of 60'

*Lunar Vision*
You gain the ability to see in the moonlight as well as the daylight. You
can see in dim light as well as full light, and in darkness, out to a range
of 30'

*Bonus Spells*

You gain three bonus spells for each level, which count as sorcerer spells
for you, and which do not count towards your known spells. These spells are
divided by phase of the moon: one spell of each level is a full moon spell,
one a new moon spell, and one a neutral spell. During the week around the
new moon, and the week of the waning moon before that, you may not cast the
full moon spell. During the week of the full moon, and the week of the
waxing moon before that, you may not cast the new moon spell. So in effect,
for half the month you have the new moon spell and half the full moon, plus
you always have access to the neutral spell.

1st Level:
New Moon: Charm Person
Full Moon: Sanctuary
Neutral: TBD

3rd Level:
New Moon: TBD
etc

*6th Level*
At 6th level, you start to be able to see and manipulate the lunar planar
tides more directly to help your magic.

When the moon is full, and for the week around it, when casting spells of
the Abjuration, Necromancy, Evocation, or Divination schools you can reduce
the cost of any metamagic by 1 min 0.

When the moon is new, and for the week around it, when casting spells of
the Enchantment, Conjuration, Illusion, or Transmutation schools you can
reduce the cost of any metamagic by 1 min 0.

You can use this feature a number of times equal to your proficiency bonus,
and regain all uses after a long rest.

[Obviously this effect is useless half the month; either I will expand so
that each version is 50% of the time, or split the spells into 3 groups
with one group for the middle, or add some other ability for the middle
point in time.. any thoughts?]

Haven't thought about 14th or higher but obviously a long way away :)

Any thoughts on race and background?

Mike

## Thu, 29 Dec 2022 12:30:21 -0500 — Matthew Rand <rand.matt@gmail.com>

Subject: Re: Lunar Sorceror

A couple of thoughts:

1. How would you feel about making the moon's blessing cantrip a spell
attack instead of a save or suck? It's probably equivalent from a math
perspective but it just feels better to be in control of whether it hits or
not.

2. For the spell lists we could look at the book for inspiration I think
the bonus spells in particular we can pull heavily from there. They use a
mix of spells including some not normally on the sorcerer's spell list if I
recall correctly. May as well not reinvent the wheel.

3. For lunar vision normally that would stack on top of dark vision but
it's slightly different, so of i choose a race with dark vision does it
become 90 or are they different? If it stacks i would just say "gain dark
vision up to 30 feet and in addition moonlight counts as daylight for you"
so the rest of it docks into the RAW more easily.

4. For the 6th level feature I love the flavor. I wonder if there's a way
to make it not totally useless in the opposite phase for a given spell? It
would feel unfortunate if the one spell I needed just was out of phase for
this. Like would it be balanced to divvy up the metamagic feats themselves
instead of the spells colleges? That way I can reduce the cost of these
metamagics on half the month and vice versa but the spells can be whatever.

Race I'm debating a bit. Originally leaning towards human, but also
considering Dwarf. Alternatively there are a couple of fun races that offer
flight which is fun but I don't know if any of them work in this world.
Background would depend a bit on the race obviously but I'm thinking some
kind of druidic / priesthood style order of the moon? Is there room for
that sort of thing? Or would I be more of a solo champion?

## Fri, 30 Dec 2022 08:50:45 -0500 — Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com>

Subject: Re: Lunar Sorceror

One random thought...

The entire party right now is CHA-focused at least a little bit... if you
wanted to play an INT-focused sorcerer I'd be happy to make that work. That
is, a sorcerer who's spellcasting stat is INT rather than CHA. An INT-based
lunar dwarven sorcerer who got magic by studying the lunar cycles would be
a good fit to the rest of the party and seems like it could be fun.

But it's just a random idea, you are welcome to keep up the CHA-trend in
the party too. That will work fine :)

Hmm that sounds fun. I didn't think of switching the spellcasting stat.

If I go mountain dwarf I think with the custom origin rules they can two +2
stat increases. Would you be ok if I use them for int and con in that case?

>> the bonus spells in particular we can pull heavily from there. They use a
>> mix of spells including some not normally on the sorcerer's spell list if I
>> recall correctly. May as well not reinvent the wheel.
>>
>>
>> I can look especially if you want to send me a screenshot of that section
>> or something. But also there is a specific Taelgar phenomenon at work here
>> — during the new moon the planar tides pull strongly at the elemental and
>> fey planes, so elemental and fey magic is stronger. During the full moon
>> the tides are pulling the divine/spiritual realms closer, so more divine
>> flavored magic is stronger. This isn’t a phenomenon that affects all
>> spellcasters but it would impact this particular subclass so I want to try
>> to align the bonus spells to that theory.
>>
>>
>> 3. For lunar vision normally that would stack on top of dark vision but
>> it's slightly different, so of i choose a race with dark vision does it
>> become 90 or are they different? If it stacks i would just say "gain dark
>> vision up to 30 feet and in addition moonlight counts as daylight for you"
>> so the rest of it docks into the RAW more easily.
>>
>>
>> Let’s discuss if it comes up. I won’t make it useless if you end up
>> taking a dwarf though.
>>
>>
>> 4. For the 6th level feature I love the flavor. I wonder if there's a way
>> to make it not totally useless in the opposite phase for a given spell? It
>> would feel unfortunate if the one spell I needed just was out of phase for
>> this. Like would it be balanced to divvy up the metamagic feats themselves
>> instead of the spells colleges? That way I can reduce the cost of these
>> metamagics on half the month and vice versa but the spells can be whatever.
>>
>>
>> I don’t love the game flavor of dividing up metamagic as the point is to
>> align the spells with the planar tides described above. But if the
>> flip-flop nature seems annoying I can brainstorm other ideas. It will be a
>> while before the 6th level feature comes into play.
>>
>> Race I'm debating a bit. Originally leaning towards human, but also
>> considering Dwarf. Alternatively there are a couple of fun races that offer
>> flight which is fun but I don't know if any of them work in this world.
>>
>>
>> I’m open to anything but human or dwarf is certainly easier to fit into
>> the world.
>>
>> Background would depend a bit on the race obviously but I'm thinking some
>> kind of druidic / priesthood style order of the moon? Is there room for
>> that sort of thing? Or would I be more of a solo champion?
>>
>>
>> Again, open to anything but a solo champion so to speak fits into the
>> plot a bit better.
>>
>> Mike
>>
>>
>> On Thu, Dec 29, 2022 at 10:54 AM Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com> wrote:
>>
>>> Matt,
>>>
>>> I haven't made up more than the initial bits, but here is what I'm
>>> thinking for lunar sorcerer. The version below is only made up through 6th
>>> level and leans heavily into different abilities for different phases of
>>> the moon. I am happy to set up a moon calendar so we know which phases of
>>> the moon are which (probably by just picking an Earth year and using that)
>>> but I can't quite decide if it will be too annoying to track.
>>>
>>> What do you think of the below? I'm still tweaking a bit so might play
>>> with the power levels a bit and totally happy to get feedback. If you like
>>> it I'll make it in D&D Beyond.
>>>
>>> *1st Level Power*
>>> You gain a new cantrip, *moon's blessing*, that allows you to call
>>> forth the power of the moon to attack your enemies, and additionally, you
>>> gain a set of bonus spells.
>>>
>>> *Moon's Blessing*
>>> You call forth the blessing of the moon to attack your enemies. You
>>> choose an enemy within 60', who must make a DEX save or take 1d8 damage.
>>> The spell damage increases by 1d8 at 5th, 11th, and 17th level.
>>>
>>> During the full moon, and the week around it, the lunar tides pull
>>> strongly on the planes of the gods, and the damage is radiant, and you gain
>>> the ability to target a second creature within 5'.
>>>
>>> During the new moon, and the week around it, the lunar tides pull
>>> strongly on the elemental planes, the damage is elemental, channeled from
>>> the elemental planes. You may choose which plane to channel when you cast
>>> the spell:
>>>
>>> From the plane of fire, the spell does fire damage.
>>> From the plane of water, the spell does cold damage.
>>> From the plane of air, the spell does thunder or lightning damage, your
>>> choice
>>> From the plane of earth, the spell does bludgeoning damage and deals
>>> double damage to object or structures
>>>
>>> In between, when the moon is a crescent, the tides are balanced, and the
>>> spell does force damage, and has a range of 90' instead of 60'
>>>
>>> *Lunar Vision*
>>> You gain the ability to see in the moonlight as well as the daylight.
>>> You can see in dim light as well as full light, and in darkness, out to a
>>> range of 30'
>>>
>>> *Bonus Spells*
>>>
>>> You gain three bonus spells for each level, which count as sorcerer
>>> spells for you, and which do not count towards your known spells. These
>>> spells are divided by phase of the moon: one spell of each level is a full
>>> moon spell, one a new moon spell, and one a neutral spell. During the week
>>> around the new moon, and the week of the waning moon before that, you may
>>> not cast the full moon spell. During the week of the full moon, and the
>>> week of the waxing moon before that, you may not cast the new moon spell.
>>> So in effect, for half the month you have the new moon spell and half the
>>> full moon, plus you always have access to the neutral spell.
>>>
>>> 1st Level:
>>> New Moon: Charm Person
>>> Full Moon: Sanctuary
>>> Neutral: TBD
>>>
>>> 3rd Level:
>>> New Moon: TBD
>>> etc
>>>
>>> *6th Level*
>>> At 6th level, you start to be able to see and manipulate the lunar
>>> planar tides more directly to help your magic.
>>>
>>> When the moon is full, and for the week around it, when casting spells
>>> of the Abjuration, Necromancy, Evocation, or Divination schools you can
>>> reduce the cost of any metamagic by 1 min 0.
>>>
>>> When the moon is new, and for the week around it, when casting spells of
>>> the Enchantment, Conjuration, Illusion, or Transmutation schools you can
>>> reduce the cost of any metamagic by 1 min 0.
>>>
>>> You can use this feature a number of times equal to your proficiency
>>> bonus, and regain all uses after a long rest.
>>>
>>> [Obviously this effect is useless half the month; either I will expand
>>> so that each version is 50% of the time, or split the spells into 3 groups
>>> with one group for the middle, or add some other ability for the middle
>>> point in time.. any thoughts?]
>>>
>>> Haven't thought about 14th or higher but obviously a long way away :)
>>>
>>> Any thoughts on race and background?
>>>
>>> Mike
>>>
>>

## Fri, 30 Dec 2022 09:21:08 -0500 — Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com>

Subject: Re: Lunar Sorceror

You should use one of the Taelgar dwarf options.

You can read more about Dwarves here:
https://www.gmbinder.com/share/-NALzGmeZSQO2O9GMtLD

Me and my brother have made two versions of dwarves on D&D Beyond for
Taelgar. There is one based on the One D&D Playtest stuff here
https://www.dndbeyond.com/races/1147048-dwarf-taelgar-playtest

and an older set of dwarf races based on each caste (Farmer, Warrior, etc).
If you enable Homebrew and then look at the homebrew dwarf options you can
see them all.

Pick either the new way or the old way, although the older way might be
easier. Either way you will only get a +2 and a +1 or three +1s for
ability scores.

Mike

On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:04 AM Matthew Rand <rand.matt@gmail.com> wrote:

>

> case?
>

## Fri, 30 Dec 2022 09:38:40 -0500 — Matthew Rand <rand.matt@gmail.com>

Subject: Re: Lunar Sorceror

Also for character hooks I'll probably read through the world bits you have
on dwarves but I'm cool going with the solo champion bit. I think that
would require being a bit unusual for a dwarf, sort of Moana is to the Sea
as this dwarf is to the moon and sky. Always sneaking out to see the moon
etc and as he ages he's reading and researching more about it and how it
works. His studies eventually lead him into magic and he by some
circumstance forms this bond with the moon.

From there I'm a bit fuzzy. Does the moon have an agenda I'm supposed to
advance? I can determine an internal reason for traveling (trying to find
others who have connected to the moon could be an easy one) but also if
once this bond is formed I'd learn anything that would motivate me that
would help

>>>>> wrote:

>>>>>> Matt,
>>>>>>
>>>>>> I haven't made up more than the initial bits, but here is what I'm
>>>>>> thinking for lunar sorcerer. The version below is only made up through 6th
>>>>>> level and leans heavily into different abilities for different phases of
>>>>>> the moon. I am happy to set up a moon calendar so we know which phases of
>>>>>> the moon are which (probably by just picking an Earth year and using that)
>>>>>> but I can't quite decide if it will be too annoying to track.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> What do you think of the below? I'm still tweaking a bit so might
>>>>>> play with the power levels a bit and totally happy to get feedback. If you
>>>>>> like it I'll make it in D&D Beyond.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> *1st Level Power*
>>>>>> You gain a new cantrip, *moon's blessing*, that allows you to call
>>>>>> forth the power of the moon to attack your enemies, and additionally, you
>>>>>> gain a set of bonus spells.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> *Moon's Blessing*
>>>>>> You call forth the blessing of the moon to attack your enemies. You
>>>>>> choose an enemy within 60', who must make a DEX save or take 1d8 damage.
>>>>>> The spell damage increases by 1d8 at 5th, 11th, and 17th level.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> During the full moon, and the week around it, the lunar tides pull
>>>>>> strongly on the planes of the gods, and the damage is radiant, and you gain
>>>>>> the ability to target a second creature within 5'.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> During the new moon, and the week around it, the lunar tides pull
>>>>>> strongly on the elemental planes, the damage is elemental, channeled from
>>>>>> the elemental planes. You may choose which plane to channel when you cast
>>>>>> the spell:
>>>>>>
>>>>>> From the plane of fire, the spell does fire damage.
>>>>>> From the plane of water, the spell does cold damage.
>>>>>> From the plane of air, the spell does thunder or lightning damage,
>>>>>> your choice
>>>>>> From the plane of earth, the spell does bludgeoning damage and deals
>>>>>> double damage to object or structures
>>>>>>
>>>>>> In between, when the moon is a crescent, the tides are balanced, and
>>>>>> the spell does force damage, and has a range of 90' instead of 60'
>>>>>>
>>>>>> *Lunar Vision*
>>>>>> You gain the ability to see in the moonlight as well as the daylight.
>>>>>> You can see in dim light as well as full light, and in darkness, out to a
>>>>>> range of 30'
>>>>>>
>>>>>> *Bonus Spells*
>>>>>>
>>>>>> You gain three bonus spells for each level, which count as sorcerer
>>>>>> spells for you, and which do not count towards your known spells. These
>>>>>> spells are divided by phase of the moon: one spell of each level is a full
>>>>>> moon spell, one a new moon spell, and one a neutral spell. During the week
>>>>>> around the new moon, and the week of the waning moon before that, you may
>>>>>> not cast the full moon spell. During the week of the full moon, and the
>>>>>> week of the waxing moon before that, you may not cast the new moon spell.
>>>>>> So in effect, for half the month you have the new moon spell and half the
>>>>>> full moon, plus you always have access to the neutral spell.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> 1st Level:
>>>>>> New Moon: Charm Person
>>>>>> Full Moon: Sanctuary
>>>>>> Neutral: TBD
>>>>>>
>>>>>> 3rd Level:
>>>>>> New Moon: TBD
>>>>>> etc
>>>>>>
>>>>>> *6th Level*
>>>>>> At 6th level, you start to be able to see and manipulate the lunar
>>>>>> planar tides more directly to help your magic.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> When the moon is full, and for the week around it, when casting
>>>>>> spells of the Abjuration, Necromancy, Evocation, or Divination schools you
>>>>>> can reduce the cost of any metamagic by 1 min 0.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> When the moon is new, and for the week around it, when casting spells
>>>>>> of the Enchantment, Conjuration, Illusion, or Transmutation schools you can
>>>>>> reduce the cost of any metamagic by 1 min 0.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> You can use this feature a number of times equal to your proficiency
>>>>>> bonus, and regain all uses after a long rest.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> [Obviously this effect is useless half the month; either I will
>>>>>> expand so that each version is 50% of the time, or split the spells into 3
>>>>>> groups with one group for the middle, or add some other ability for the
>>>>>> middle point in time.. any thoughts?]
>>>>>>
>>>>>> Haven't thought about 14th or higher but obviously a long way away :)
>>>>>>
>>>>>> Any thoughts on race and background?
>>>>>>
>>>>>> Mike
>>>>>>
>>>>>

## Fri, 30 Dec 2022 10:47:13 -0500 — Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com>

Subject: Re: Lunar Sorceror

I want to keep the moon mysterious and a bit vague, in the spirit of the
lore I sent you. That is, it isn't a personality that talks to you or has
wishes or desires that you really know. It would more be a source of power,
in that you have learned to tap into the magical influence of the moon to
create spells. You'd probably have a better sense of the mechanics of the
moon than its personality, which is entirely a mystery to you.

There are many many stories about the origins and true nature of the moon
and you would have little sense of what is or is not true. The bit you
would understand a bit better is the nature of planar tides. The moon
influences how the planes interact with Taelgar in an important way. When
it is full, it pulls on the spiritual realms (the homes of the gods, and
places like Arborea, Pandemonium, and the Nine Hells -- the Outer Planes in
traditional D&D sense) and draws them closer to Taelgar itself. When it is
new, the tides pull the opposite way, so to speak, and it pulls the
elemental planes, the Feywild, and the Shadowfell and related planes
closer.

You've learned to tap into whatever magical power of the moon is driving
this pulling and shifting of the planar balance and channel it into spells.
So the source of your magic is almost not even the moon per se, but rather
than energy of the magical tides.

If it works for your character conception the vision I had of a lunar
sorcerer was somewhat more scientific than mystical, so to speak.

There are communities or groups that do have a more mystical connection to
various legends and myths around the moon, and there would also be
astronomers and scholars who study the moon, although neither of these are
likely to be big in the dwarf community.

I was trying to avoid making up too much detail about the moon this week
but I can if you feel you would rather not go the science-y route and want
something more mystical.

A couple of ideas for motivations....

You could be seeking information or things - there is a small dwarven
community in Clenseau, and it is known for its smithing.

** Maybe there is a dwarf there who specializes in rare metals that fell
from the sky and you want to meet them.
** Or a dwarf or human who builds strange devices, and you heard a rumor of
some telescope like thing that they were building.
** Or maybe an eccentric human who has been mapping the phases and orbit of
the moon for fifty years that you heard of somehow.
** Or there could be a rumor of a small god (a *kestavo*, one of the
spirits that humans used to worship that are now mostly gone) that was the
patron of a small spring that glowed with a mystical light in the
moonlight, and you are looking for it, hoping to see if you could commune
with the spirit and learn more about the moon.

You could have a non-moon connection to the dwarven community. A brother,
sister, cousin, uncle, aunt, etc who lives there, and who is going to give
you a place to live/be. If for example you were a young dwarf who wanted to
leave the mountains, heading somewhere like Clenseau could be a sensible
place to be going -- just because you want to settle somewhere where you
can see stars. (Although it is worth pointing out that dwarven cities are
not entirely underground,

You could be seeking money, and heard of someone in Clenseau offering a job
or some sort of funding, which you need for your research.

There is also no reason you have to be young. Maybe you came to the
moon-research in your mid-life, and have a personal connection to Clenseau
from a hundred years ago. You could be returning after spending some time
in the mountains.

You could be chasing someone or something -- a friend / sibling / etc who
is lost and the trail leads you to Clenseau.

I can come up with more ideas if nothing above inspires.

## Fri, 30 Dec 2022 11:52:04 -0500 — Matthew Rand <rand.matt@gmail.com>

Subject: Re: Lunar Sorceror

Ok let me know how this lands for you.

Durgil was born some 200 years ago and never quite fit into the role his
family and clan made for him. When he went through his naming ritual he
earned his Thuhr as one of the Travelers, followers of Maganna, but
something was always odd about his experience as he swore there was a
second figure in the shadows when he was given his Thuhr. His family told
him no one has more than one Thuhr and to put it out of his mind. He
settled into a role of a traveling merchant for the people of Nidzahar
making deals for mineral sales and crafted works and acquiring food and
other finer things from the far and wide. His travels as welcome as they
were gave him much time to think and he often found himself staring up at
the stars and thinking of the world beyond the dwarven halls he was
supposed to be missing. Far from being comfortable when he returned from a
trip he tended to itch to get back out on the road but never really able to
articulate why.

One day after a particularly long trip he was in the foothills of the
mountain and found himself for a second time transported to the Heart of
the Mountain. This time a different figure bade him join a new Thuhr and
take on a new name. He was bid to study with the Runecrafters and scholars
and take on a new name of Izgil. Once accepted into the new Thuhr he was to
study and learn the ways of the moon and bring his knowledge to any who
would listen.

Upsetting the normal order of things and alienating his friends for over
100 years, Izgil changed Thuhr and began studying the moon and it's history
and mechanisms. He learned to channel its own energies for rudimentary
spells and even began working on new magical theory for how the
metaphysical tides of the moon could be exploited for magical means. But he
quickly found the knowledge available ran out and was rudimentary at best.
He needed new angles and new sources of knowledge. And so he set out for
the road again and has been traveling for a few years now. He had been
refining his theories and gathering more stories and details. He tends to
not discriminate between mysticism, science and lore as he believes there
is just as much truth potentially is magical approaches as in the science
of the moon though he approaches each as a purely academic study.

Most recently he learned of the crafters in the settlement of Clenseau who
are said to be making a device that would allow him to inspect the details
of the surface of the moon itself and so he goes to learn more and broaden
his knowledge, happy to once again be sleeping under the stars...

> with the spirit and learn more about the moon.
>
> You could have a non-moon connection to the dwarven community. A brother,
> sister, cousin, uncle, aunt, etc who lives there, and who is going to give
> you a place to live/be. If for example you were a young dwarf who wanted to
> leave the mountains, heading somewhere like Clenseau could be a sensible
> place to be going -- just because you want to settle somewhere where you
> can see stars. (Although it is worth pointing out that dwarven cities are
> not entirely underground,
>
> You could be seeking money, and heard of someone in Clenseau offering a
> job or some sort of funding, which you need for your research.
>
> There is also no reason you have to be young. Maybe you came to the
> moon-research in your mid-life, and have a personal connection to Clenseau
> from a hundred years ago. You could be returning after spending some time
> in the mountains.
>
> You could be chasing someone or something -- a friend / sibling / etc who
> is lost and the trail leads you to Clenseau.
>
> I can come up with more ideas if nothing above inspires.
>
>
> On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:38 AM Matthew Rand <rand.matt@gmail.com> wrote:
>
>> Also for character hooks I'll probably read through the world bits you
>> have on dwarves but I'm cool going with the solo champion bit. I think that
>> would require being a bit unusual for a dwarf, sort of Moana is to the Sea
>> as this dwarf is to the moon and sky. Always sneaking out to see the moon
>> etc and as he ages he's reading and researching more about it and how it
>> works. His studies eventually lead him into magic and he by some
>> circumstance forms this bond with the moon.
>>
>> From there I'm a bit fuzzy. Does the moon have an agenda I'm supposed to
>> advance? I can determine an internal reason for traveling (trying to find
>> others who have connected to the moon could be an easy one) but also if
>> once this bond is formed I'd learn anything that would motivate me that
>> would help
>>
>> On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:30 AM Matthew Rand <rand.matt@gmail.com> wrote:
>>
>>> Awesome, thanks!
>>>
>>> On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:21 AM Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com> wrote:
>>>
>>>> You should use one of the Taelgar dwarf options.
>>>>
>>>> You can read more about Dwarves here:
>>>> https://www.gmbinder.com/share/-NALzGmeZSQO2O9GMtLD
>>>>
>>>> Me and my brother have made two versions of dwarves on D&D Beyond for
>>>> Taelgar. There is one based on the One D&D Playtest stuff here
>>>> https://www.dndbeyond.com/races/1147048-dwarf-taelgar-playtest
>>>>
>>>> and an older set of dwarf races based on each caste (Farmer, Warrior,
>>>> etc). If you enable Homebrew and then look at the homebrew dwarf options
>>>> you can see them all.
>>>>
>>>> Pick either the new way or the old way, although the older way might be
>>>> easier. Either way you will only get a +2 and a +1 or three +1s for
>>>> ability scores.
>>>>
>>>> Mike
>>>>
>>>> On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:04 AM Matthew Rand <rand.matt@gmail.com>
>>>> wrote:
>>>>
>>>>> Hmm that sounds fun. I didn't think of switching the spellcasting
>>>>> stat. Good idea!
>>>>>
>>>>> If I go mountain dwarf I think with the custom origin rules they can
>>>>> two +2 stat increases. Would you be ok if I use them for int and con in
>>>>> that case?
>>>>>
>>>>> On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 8:50 AM Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com>
>>>>> wrote:
>>>>>
>>>>>> One random thought...
>>>>>>
>>>>>> The entire party right now is CHA-focused at least a little bit... if
>>>>>> you wanted to play an INT-focused sorcerer I'd be happy to make that work.
>>>>>> That is, a sorcerer who's spellcasting stat is INT rather than CHA. An
>>>>>> INT-based lunar dwarven sorcerer who got magic by studying the lunar cycles
>>>>>> would be a good fit to the rest of the party and seems like it could be fun.
>>>>>>
>>>>>> But it's just a random idea, you are welcome to keep up the CHA-trend
>>>>>> in the party too. That will work fine :)
>>>>>>
>>>>>>
>>>>>>
>>>>>> On Thu, Dec 29, 2022 at 2:07 PM Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com>
>>>>>> wrote:
>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> On Dec 29, 2022, at 12:30 PM, Matthew Rand <rand.matt@gmail.com>
 >>>>>>> wrote:
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> A couple of thoughts:
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> 1. How would you feel about making the moon's blessing cantrip a
 >>>>>>> spell attack instead of a save or suck? It's probably equivalent from a
 >>>>>>> math perspective but it just feels better to be in control of whether it
 >>>>>>> hits or not.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> Fine with me.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> 2. For the spell lists we could look at the book for inspiration I
 >>>>>>> think the bonus spells in particular we can pull heavily from there. They
 >>>>>>> use a mix of spells including some not normally on the sorcerer's spell
 >>>>>>> list if I recall correctly. May as well not reinvent the wheel.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> I can look especially if you want to send me a screenshot of that
 >>>>>>> section or something. But also there is a specific Taelgar phenomenon at
 >>>>>>> work here — during the new moon the planar tides pull strongly at the
 >>>>>>> elemental and fey planes, so elemental and fey magic is stronger. During
 >>>>>>> the full moon the tides are pulling the divine/spiritual realms closer, so
 >>>>>>> more divine flavored magic is stronger. This isn’t a phenomenon that
 >>>>>>> affects all spellcasters but it would impact this particular subclass so I
 >>>>>>> want to try to align the bonus spells to that theory.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> 3. For lunar vision normally that would stack on top of dark vision
 >>>>>>> but it's slightly different, so of i choose a race with dark vision does it
 >>>>>>> become 90 or are they different? If it stacks i would just say "gain dark
 >>>>>>> vision up to 30 feet and in addition moonlight counts as daylight for you"
 >>>>>>> so the rest of it docks into the RAW more easily.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> Let’s discuss if it comes up. I won’t make it useless if you end up
 >>>>>>> taking a dwarf though.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> 4. For the 6th level feature I love the flavor. I wonder if there's
 >>>>>>> a way to make it not totally useless in the opposite phase for a given
 >>>>>>> spell? It would feel unfortunate if the one spell I needed just was out of
 >>>>>>> phase for this. Like would it be balanced to divvy up the metamagic feats
 >>>>>>> themselves instead of the spells colleges? That way I can reduce the cost
 >>>>>>> of these metamagics on half the month and vice versa but the spells can be
 >>>>>>> whatever.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> I don’t love the game flavor of dividing up metamagic as the point
 >>>>>>> is to align the spells with the planar tides described above. But if the
 >>>>>>> flip-flop nature seems annoying I can brainstorm other ideas. It will be a
 >>>>>>> while before the 6th level feature comes into play.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> Race I'm debating a bit. Originally leaning towards human, but also
 >>>>>>> considering Dwarf. Alternatively there are a couple of fun races that offer
 >>>>>>> flight which is fun but I don't know if any of them work in this world.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> I’m open to anything but human or dwarf is certainly easier to fit
 >>>>>>> into the world.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> Background would depend a bit on the race obviously but I'm thinking
 >>>>>>> some kind of druidic / priesthood style order of the moon? Is there room
 >>>>>>> for that sort of thing? Or would I be more of a solo champion?
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> Again, open to anything but a solo champion so to speak fits into
 >>>>>>> the plot a bit better.
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> Mike
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>> On Thu, Dec 29, 2022 at 10:54 AM Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com>
 >>>>>>> wrote:
 >>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> Matt,
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> I haven't made up more than the initial bits, but here is what I'm
 >>>>>>>> thinking for lunar sorcerer. The version below is only made up through 6th
 >>>>>>>> level and leans heavily into different abilities for different phases of
 >>>>>>>> the moon. I am happy to set up a moon calendar so we know which phases of
 >>>>>>>> the moon are which (probably by just picking an Earth year and using that)
 >>>>>>>> but I can't quite decide if it will be too annoying to track.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> What do you think of the below? I'm still tweaking a bit so might
 >>>>>>>> play with the power levels a bit and totally happy to get feedback. If you
 >>>>>>>> like it I'll make it in D&D Beyond.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> *1st Level Power*
 >>>>>>>> You gain a new cantrip, *moon's blessing*, that allows you to call
 >>>>>>>> forth the power of the moon to attack your enemies, and additionally, you
 >>>>>>>> gain a set of bonus spells.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> *Moon's Blessing*
 >>>>>>>> You call forth the blessing of the moon to attack your enemies. You
 >>>>>>>> choose an enemy within 60', who must make a DEX save or take 1d8 damage.
 >>>>>>>> The spell damage increases by 1d8 at 5th, 11th, and 17th level.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> During the full moon, and the week around it, the lunar tides pull
 >>>>>>>> strongly on the planes of the gods, and the damage is radiant, and you gain
 >>>>>>>> the ability to target a second creature within 5'.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> During the new moon, and the week around it, the lunar tides pull
 >>>>>>>> strongly on the elemental planes, the damage is elemental, channeled from
 >>>>>>>> the elemental planes. You may choose which plane to channel when you cast
 >>>>>>>> the spell:
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> From the plane of fire, the spell does fire damage.
 >>>>>>>> From the plane of water, the spell does cold damage.
 >>>>>>>> From the plane of air, the spell does thunder or lightning damage,
 >>>>>>>> your choice
 >>>>>>>> From the plane of earth, the spell does bludgeoning damage and
 >>>>>>>> deals double damage to object or structures
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> In between, when the moon is a crescent, the tides are balanced,
 >>>>>>>> and the spell does force damage, and has a range of 90' instead of 60'
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> *Lunar Vision*
 >>>>>>>> You gain the ability to see in the moonlight as well as the
 >>>>>>>> daylight. You can see in dim light as well as full light, and in darkness,
 >>>>>>>> out to a range of 30'
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> *Bonus Spells*
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> You gain three bonus spells for each level, which count as sorcerer
 >>>>>>>> spells for you, and which do not count towards your known spells. These
 >>>>>>>> spells are divided by phase of the moon: one spell of each level is a full
 >>>>>>>> moon spell, one a new moon spell, and one a neutral spell. During the week
 >>>>>>>> around the new moon, and the week of the waning moon before that, you may
 >>>>>>>> not cast the full moon spell. During the week of the full moon, and the
 >>>>>>>> week of the waxing moon before that, you may not cast the new moon spell.
 >>>>>>>> So in effect, for half the month you have the new moon spell and half the
 >>>>>>>> full moon, plus you always have access to the neutral spell.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> 1st Level:
 >>>>>>>> New Moon: Charm Person
 >>>>>>>> Full Moon: Sanctuary
 >>>>>>>> Neutral: TBD
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> 3rd Level:
 >>>>>>>> New Moon: TBD
 >>>>>>>> etc
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> *6th Level*
 >>>>>>>> At 6th level, you start to be able to see and manipulate the lunar
 >>>>>>>> planar tides more directly to help your magic.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> When the moon is full, and for the week around it, when casting
 >>>>>>>> spells of the Abjuration, Necromancy, Evocation, or Divination schools you
 >>>>>>>> can reduce the cost of any metamagic by 1 min 0.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> When the moon is new, and for the week around it, when casting
 >>>>>>>> spells of the Enchantment, Conjuration, Illusion, or Transmutation schools
 >>>>>>>> you can reduce the cost of any metamagic by 1 min 0.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> You can use this feature a number of times equal to your
 >>>>>>>> proficiency bonus, and regain all uses after a long rest.
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> [Obviously this effect is useless half the month; either I will
 >>>>>>>> expand so that each version is 50% of the time, or split the spells into 3
 >>>>>>>> groups with one group for the middle, or add some other ability for the
 >>>>>>>> middle point in time.. any thoughts?]
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> Haven't thought about 14th or higher but obviously a long way away
 >>>>>>>> :)
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> Any thoughts on race and background?
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>> Mike
 >>>>>>>>
 >>>>>>>

## Fri, 30 Dec 2022 11:57:06 -0500 — Mike Sackton <msackton@gmail.com>

Subject: Fwd: Lunar Sorceror

(HTML source)

<html><head><meta http-equiv="content-type" content="text/html; charset=utf-8"></head><body dir="auto">Any comments?<br><br><div dir="ltr">Sent from my iPhone</div><div dir="ltr"><br>Begin forwarded message:<br><br></div><blockquote type="cite"><div dir="ltr"><b>From:</b> Matthew Rand &lt;rand.matt@gmail.com&gt;<br><b>Date:</b> December 30, 2022 at 11:52:16 AM EST<br><b>To:</b> Mike Sackton &lt;msackton@gmail.com&gt;<br><b>Subject:</b> <b>Re: Lunar Sorceror</b><br><br></div></blockquote><blockquote type="cite"><div dir="ltr">﻿<div><div><div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)">Ok let me know how this lands for you.&nbsp;</div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)"><br></div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)">Durgil was born some 200 years ago and never quite fit into the role his family and clan made for him. When he went through his naming ritual he earned his Thuhr as one of the Travelers, followers of Maganna, but something was always odd about his experience as he swore there was a second figure in the shadows when he was given his Thuhr. His family told him no one has more than one Thuhr and to put it out of his mind. He settled into a role of a traveling merchant for the people of Nidzahar making deals for mineral sales and crafted works and acquiring food and other finer things from the far and wide. His travels as welcome as they were gave him much time to think and he often found himself staring up at the stars and thinking of the world beyond the dwarven halls he was supposed to be missing. Far from being comfortable when he returned from a trip he tended to itch to get back out on the road but never really able to articulate why.</div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)"><br></div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)">One day after a particularly long trip he was in the foothills of the mountain and found himself for a second time transported to the Heart of the Mountain. This time a different figure bade him join a new Thuhr and take on a new name. He was bid to study with the Runecrafters and scholars and take on a new name of Izgil. Once accepted into the new Thuhr he was to study and learn the ways of the moon and bring his knowledge to any who would listen.&nbsp;</div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)"><br></div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)">Upsetting the normal order of things and alienating his friends for over 100 years, Izgil changed Thuhr and began studying the moon and it's history and mechanisms. He learned to channel its own energies for rudimentary spells and even began working on new magical theory for how the metaphysical tides of the moon could be exploited for magical means. But he quickly found the knowledge available ran out and was rudimentary at best. He needed new angles and new sources of knowledge. And so he set out for the road again and has been traveling for a few years now. He had been refining his theories and gathering more stories and details. He tends to not discriminate between mysticism, science and lore as he believes there is just as much truth potentially is magical approaches as in the science of the moon though he approaches each as a purely academic study.&nbsp;</div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)"><br></div><div dir="auto" style="border-color:rgb(0,0,0);color:rgb(0,0,0)">Most recently he learned of the crafters in the settlement of Clenseau who are said to be making a device that would allow him to inspect the details of the surface of the moon itself and so he goes to learn more and broaden his knowledge, happy to once again be sleeping under the stars...</div></div></div></div><div><div><div><br><div class="gmail_quote"><div dir="ltr" class="gmail_attr">On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 10:47 AM Mike Sackton &lt;<a href="mailto:msackton@gmail.com" target="_blank">msackton@gmail.com</a>&gt; wrote:<br></div><blockquote class="gmail_quote" style="margin:0px 0px 0px 0.8ex;border-left-width:1px;border-left-style:solid;padding-left:1ex;border-left-color:rgb(204,204,204)"><div dir="ltr">I want to keep the moon mysterious and a bit vague, in the spirit&nbsp;of the lore I sent you. That is, it isn't a personality that talks to you or has wishes or desires&nbsp;that you really know. It would more be a source of power, in that you have learned to tap into the magical influence of the moon to create spells. You'd probably have a better sense of the mechanics of the moon than its personality, which is entirely a mystery to you.<div><br></div><div>There are many many stories about the origins and true nature of the moon and you would have little sense of what is or is not true. The bit you would understand a bit better is the nature of planar tides. The moon influences how the planes interact with Taelgar in an important way. When it is full, it pulls on the spiritual realms (the homes of the gods, and places like Arborea, Pandemonium, and the Nine Hells -- the Outer Planes in traditional D&amp;D sense) and draws them closer to Taelgar itself. When it is new, the tides pull the opposite way, so to speak, and it pulls the elemental planes, the Feywild, and the Shadowfell and related planes closer.&nbsp;</div><div><br></div><div>You've learned to tap into whatever magical power of the moon is driving this pulling and shifting of the planar balance and channel it into spells. So the source of your magic is almost not even the moon per se, but rather than energy of the magical tides.&nbsp;</div><div><br></div><div>If it works for your character conception the vision I had of a lunar sorcerer was somewhat more scientific than mystical, so to speak.&nbsp;</div><div><br></div><div>There are communities or groups that do have a more mystical connection to various legends and myths around the moon, and there would also be astronomers and scholars who study the moon, although neither of these are likely to be big in the dwarf community.&nbsp;</div><div><br></div><div>I was trying to avoid making up too much detail about the moon this week but I can if you feel you would rather not go the science-y route and want something more mystical.</div><div><br></div><div>A couple of ideas for motivations....</div><div><br></div><div>You could be seeking information or things - there is a small dwarven community in Clenseau, and it is known for its smithing.&nbsp;</div><div><br></div><div>** Maybe there is a dwarf there who specializes in rare metals that fell from the sky and you want to meet them.&nbsp;</div><div>** Or a dwarf or human who builds strange devices, and you heard a rumor of some telescope like thing that they were building.&nbsp;</div><div>** Or maybe an eccentric human who has been mapping the phases and orbit of the moon for fifty years that you heard of somehow.&nbsp;</div><div>** Or there could be a rumor of a small god (a <i>kestavo</i>, one of the spirits that humans used to worship that are now mostly gone) that was the patron of a small spring that glowed with a mystical light in the moonlight, and you are looking for it, hoping to see if you could commune with the spirit and learn more about the moon.</div><div><br></div><div>You could have a non-moon connection to the dwarven community. A brother, sister, cousin, uncle, aunt, etc who lives there, and who is going to give you a place to live/be. If for example you were a young dwarf who wanted to leave the mountains, heading somewhere like Clenseau could be a sensible place to be going -- just because you want to settle somewhere where you can see stars. (Although it is worth pointing out that dwarven cities are not entirely underground,&nbsp;</div><div><br></div><div>You could be seeking money, and heard of someone in Clenseau&nbsp;offering a job or some sort of funding, which you need for your research.</div><div><br></div><div>There is also no reason you have to be young. Maybe you came to the moon-research in your mid-life, and have a personal connection to Clenseau from a hundred years ago. You could be returning after spending some time in the mountains.</div><div><br></div><div>You could be chasing someone or something -- a friend / sibling / etc who is lost and the trail leads you to Clenseau.</div><div><br></div><div>I can come up with more ideas if nothing above inspires.</div><div><br></div></div><br><div class="gmail_quote"><div dir="ltr" class="gmail_attr">On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:38 AM Matthew Rand &lt;<a href="mailto:rand.matt@gmail.com" target="_blank">rand.matt@gmail.com</a>&gt; wrote:<br></div><blockquote class="gmail_quote" style="margin:0px 0px 0px 0.8ex;border-left-width:1px;border-left-style:solid;padding-left:1ex;border-left-color:rgb(204,204,204)"><div dir="auto">Also for character hooks I'll probably read through the world bits you have on dwarves but I'm cool going with the solo champion bit. I think that would require being a bit unusual for a dwarf, sort of Moana is to the Sea as this dwarf is to the moon and sky. Always sneaking out to see the moon etc and as he ages he's reading and researching more about it and how it works. His studies eventually lead him into magic and he by some circumstance forms this bond with the moon.&nbsp;</div><div dir="auto"><br></div><div dir="auto">From there I'm a bit fuzzy. Does the moon have an agenda I'm supposed to advance? I can determine an internal reason for traveling (trying to find others who have connected to the moon could be an easy one) but also if once this bond is formed I'd learn anything that would motivate me that would help</div><div><br><div class="gmail_quote"><div dir="ltr" class="gmail_attr">On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:30 AM Matthew Rand &lt;<a href="mailto:rand.matt@gmail.com" target="_blank">rand.matt@gmail.com</a>&gt; wrote:<br></div><blockquote class="gmail_quote" style="margin:0px 0px 0px 0.8ex;border-left-width:1px;border-left-style:solid;padding-left:1ex;border-left-color:rgb(204,204,204)"><div>Awesome, thanks!</div><div><br><div class="gmail_quote"><div dir="ltr" class="gmail_attr">On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:21 AM Mike Sackton &lt;<a href="mailto:msackton@gmail.com" target="_blank">msackton@gmail.com</a>&gt; wrote:<br></div><blockquote class="gmail_quote" style="margin:0px 0px 0px 0.8ex;border-left-width:1px;border-left-style:solid;padding-left:1ex;border-left-color:rgb(204,204,204)"><div dir="ltr">You should use one of the Taelgar dwarf&nbsp;options.<div><br></div><div>You can read more about Dwarves here:&nbsp;<a href="https://www.gmbinder.com/share/-NALzGmeZSQO2O9GMtLD" target="_blank">https://www.gmbinder.com/share/-NALzGmeZSQO2O9GMtLD</a></div><div><br></div><div>Me and my brother have made two versions of dwarves on D&amp;D&nbsp;Beyond for Taelgar. Either way you will only get a&nbsp;+2 and a&nbsp;+1 or three&nbsp;+1s for ability&nbsp;scores.</div></div><div dir="ltr"><div><br></div><div>Mike</div></div><br><div class="gmail_quote"><div dir="ltr" class="gmail_attr">On Fri, Dec 30, 2022 at 9:04 AM Matthew Rand &lt;<a href="mailto:rand.matt@gmail.com" target="_blank">rand.matt@gmail.com</a>&gt; wrote:<br></div><blockquote class="gmail_quote" style="margin:0px 0px 0px 0.8ex;border-left-width:1px;border-left-style:solid;padding-left:1ex;border-left-color:rgb(204,204,204)"><div dir="auto">Hmm that sounds fun.
